原油だけでなく石油精製品や天然ガスなどにも供給制約。インフレ圧力は長期化
◼ 原油高に加え、製油能力逼迫に伴うクラック・スプレッドの上昇もグローバルなインフレを助長する懸念
― クラック・スプレッド（石油精製品と原油の価格差）は例年を大きく上回る水準。戦争の影響を背景に中東・ロシアからの
輸出が減少していること等が影響
◼ 代替ルートの乏しい天然ガスや肥料（尿素など）の供給懸念は継続。先物価格は高止まり
― 天然ガスでは中東依存度が高い欧州やアジアで価格が上昇。欧州ガス価格（TTF）は前年比で約2.4倍まで急騰

― 先物価格も需給ひっ迫継続を見越して高止まり。中東情勢次第では、天然ガス価格がさらに上振れる可能性も

◼ 原油供給回復の遅れ

◼ 設備・資材需要の増加
◼ 電力・人材需要の増加
◼ 資金需要の拡大

インフレ長期化：エネルギー供給ショックや投資需要がインフレ圧力に
◼ ホルムズ海峡の封鎖長期化により、エネルギー価格は当面高止まり
― 開戦以降、グローバルCPIのエネルギー指数は前年比約15％まで上昇。原油や石化製品などの高騰が、航空運賃など
のサービス価格や、輸送コストの上昇を通じた幅広い財価格に波及
◼ 旺盛なAI関連需要が物価を押し上げ
― データセンター投資需要を受けメモリー価格が高騰。PC・スマホ等の最終製品価格へも波及
― 需要拡大に伴う電力価格の上昇も相まって、供給ショックと投資需要が同時に物価を押し上げ

（注） 政府機関閉鎖による米CPIデータ欠損のため、2025年10月は欠値
（出所） OECDより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
グローバルCPI（OECD加盟国平均） AI関連インフレ指標（メモリ単価）

（出所） 韓国産業通商資源部、CEICより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

高金利の常態化：政策金利に加え、タームプレミアムも金利を押し上げ
◼ 主要国・地域の中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ移行
― ホルムズ危機長期化や投資需要の強さに伴うインフレ圧力の高まりを受け、日米欧の中銀はいずれも9月に利上げ
◼ 日本以外の主要国においても、政策金利の上昇織り込みや財政懸念を背景に長期金利は上昇
― 米国では財政懸念に加え、足元では政策金利の上昇織り込みが長期金利を押し上げ

### Page 13

成長・負担の偏在が広げる「耐性格差」―4つの緊張関係
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
②企業・家計間の緊張
生産性上昇
企業収益増加
遅れる賃金
・所得への波及
③企業間の緊張
大企業
／AI・戦略分野
中小企業
／非AI・戦略分野
④家計間の緊張
資産保有層
／高所得層
資産非保有層
／中低所得層
①国家間の緊張
技術／資本／資源
を持つ国
技術／資本／資源
を持たない国
→
成長の実感乏しく、社会的受容性が低下
→
保護主義・産業政策競争が加速
→
消費・資産形成力の差が累積
→
投資余力の差が次の成長機会を左右
格差は一時的か、固定化・自己増幅するか
（耐性格差が強まる中での日本の立ち位置は？）
投資主導経済＋インフレ＋高金利 拡大する耐性格差成長・負担の偏在
偏在化する成長の果実「新常態」下の金融市場中東危機長期化、加速するAI・レジリエンス投資

### Page 14

「耐性格差」は固定化するのか―成長の持続性を見極める3つの評価軸
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
見極める軸 ポイント 代表指標 含意
波及 成長の果実は広がるか
◼ 生産性
◼ 実質賃金
◼ AI導入率
◼ 中小企業収益
波及すれば成長の裾野が拡大
しなければ成長の偏りが固定化
機会 「持たざる側」も次の成長機
会に参加できるか
◼ 労働移動
◼ 新規参入
◼ 資金調達格差
◼ 人的資本投資
参加できれば格差は流動的
できなければ勝者・敗者が固定化
底上げ 格差が残る中でも、弱い主
体の生活水準は改善するか
◼ ジニ係数（再分配）
◼ リスキリング
◼ 資産形成
◼ 社会移動
改善すれば社会的受容性を維持
しなければ分断圧力が強まる
問われるのは格差そのものではなく、偏在が成長の持続性を損なうか
⇒成長の果実が広がり、次の機会と生活水準の改善につながるかが焦点
評
価
軸
と
そ
の
含
意
問
題
設
定
「波及・機会・底上げ」を通じて、成長の持続性をモニタリング
偏在化する成長の果実「新常態」下の金融市場中東危機長期化、加速するAI・レジリエンス投資

### Page 15

参考①（国家間）：競争力の基盤は国ごとに偏在、資源高は日本に逆風
◼ 国家の競争力は、資源・製造能力・技術力・資金動員力・市場規模など複数の基盤に左右
― 日本は製造能力・技術力に強みを持つ一方、資源面の脆弱性から資源高局面では交易条件が悪化。また、潤沢な資金
（金融資産）を有しているものの、国内成長分野への資金動員力に課題
― 台湾・韓国では資源高で輸入価格が上昇する一方、半導体などAI関連財の価格上昇が輸出価格を押し上げ、交易条件
を下支え
国家の競争力を支える5つの基盤

参考②（企業・家計間）：生産性向上に遅れる賃金、低下する労働分配率
◼ 日本では時間当たり生産性が上昇する一方、1人当たり実質賃金の伸びは限定的
― 働き方改革など労働時間の短縮も両者の乖離に影響。交易条件悪化による所得流出も実質賃金を下押しする一因に
― 対して、AIブームの恩恵が大きい米国や韓国では、労働生産性と実質賃金がともに上昇する好循環

◼ 日本の労働分配率は主要国・地域と比較しても低下傾向。企業収益の賃金への還元が今後の焦点
― 米国や韓国においても足元で低下がみられるが、AI需要の拡大による企業収益の伸びが主因
― 日本では雇用者報酬の伸びが緩やかであり、生産性向上の成果を賃金に繋げられるかどうかが課題
（注） 労働生産性は総労働時間あたりの実質GDP、実質賃金は雇用者1人あたり
（出所） OECDより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
労働生産性と実質賃金 主要国・地域における労働分配率の推移
（注） 自営業者の労働所得を調整した雇用者報酬の名目GDP比。韓国の2024年以降は
OECDのデータを用いて延長
（出所） 欧州委員会、OECDより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 17

参考③（企業間）：金利上昇への耐性は企業規模・業種で大きな格差
◼ 日本企業の投下資本利益率（ROIC）は規模・業種間で大きな格差
― 総じてみれば、企業規模が小さいほどROICが低い傾向（中堅企業は例外）。特に小規模企業は1.5％と低水準
― 業種別では、製造業の輸送用機械や、非製造業の不動産、電力・ガス、運輸・郵便、医療・福祉などでROICが低い傾向。
なお、情報通信や宿泊・飲食は大企業のROICが相対的に高い一方、小規模企業が低く、業種内の格差が大きい
◼ インタレストカバレッジレシオ（ICR）も規模・業種間で格差。ROIC・ICRともに低い規模・業種ほど金利上昇に脆弱な構造
― ICRも規模が小さいほど低位（中堅企業は例外）。業種別では、不動産、電力・ガス、運輸・郵便、医療・福祉等が低水準
（注） 投下資本利益率（ROIC）＝税引き後営業利益÷（有利子負債＋自己資本）。大企業
は資本金10億円以上、中堅企業は1億円以上10億円未満、中小企業は1千万円以上
1億円未満、小規模企業は1千万円未満

（出所） 財務省「法人企業統計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（注） インタレストカバレッジレシオ（ICR）＝営業利益÷支払利息。大企業は資本金10億円
以上、中堅企業は1億円以上10億円未満、中小企業は1千万円以上1億円未満、小

低所得世帯 高所得世帯
参考④（家計間）：金融資産を保有する層と持たざる層の間でインフレ耐性に格差
◼ 所得階級によって資産形成余力に格差。低所得世帯ほど資産形成が進みにくく、インフレ耐性が脆弱
― 高所得世帯では貯蓄余力が大きい一方、低所得世帯では物価高による生活費の増加が重石となり、貯蓄率は低水準
― 低所得世帯では金融資産の蓄積が進みにくく、インフレによる実質所得の目減りを資産所得で補う余地も限定的
◼ 資産形成余力に加え、資産選択も二極化。インフレ下では資産格差が拡大し、インフレ耐性の差も広がりやすい構造
― 余裕資金のある高所得世帯は収益性重視のリスク資産投資に積極的。一方、中低所得世帯はリスク資産投資に消極的
― インフレは資産形成余力と資産選択の違いを増幅させ、資産格差とインフレ耐性の格差がさらに拡大する可能性
（注） 勤労者世帯の平均貯蓄率。平均貯蓄率＝貯蓄純増 ÷ 可処分所得 × 100。対象

世帯・項目の違いにより、GDPベースの貯蓄率とは一致しない
（出所） 総務省「家計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
所得別にみた貯蓄率（2021～25年平均） 年収別にみた金融資産の選択基準（2021～25年）
（注） 対象は金融資産を保有する二人以上世帯
（出所） J-FLEC「家計の金融行動に関する世論調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（１）米国：AIによる景気押し上げは当面継続。物価高から FRBは一時的に政策金利を引き上げへ
◼ 2026・27年の実質GDP成長率はいずれも前年比＋2.2％と予想
― 設備投資は、2027年入り後に巡航速度並みの伸び率へ回帰。ハイパースケーラーは投資効率の低下を意識し、AIの普
及ペースを見極めながら投資を継続
― 個人消費は減税政策や資産効果により底堅く推移。2027年入り後はインフレ減速に伴う実質所得の改善も支えに
◼ FRBは関税引き上げや原油高によるインフレ上振れリスクを警戒し、2026年内に追加で1回の利上げ。関税・原油高の影
響が一巡した後、 物価の十分な減速が確認される2027年後半以降に2回の利下げを予想
（注） 網掛けはみずほ総合研究所による予測値

米国：設備投資が景気のドライバーとなるなか、 AIブームの持続性が焦点に
◼ 足元の景気は堅調。引き続き、AIを中心とする設備投資が景気のけん引役に
― 4～6月期のGDP成長率は前期比年率＋2.2％と巡航速度を上回る結果。設備投資に加え、 株高に伴う資産効果や

米国：低採用・低離職で人材獲得競争は限定的。足元の物価上昇には賃金以外の要因が影響

◼ 2026年後半は、熱波を受けた生産抑制やインフレによる消費下押しなど逆風はありつつも、財政拡張が下支え

### Page 32

欧州：熱波による河川水位低下が景気下押し要因に
◼ 河川の水位低下による生産下振れが7～9月期の成長率を下押し
― 高温と少雨を背景に欧州各地では河川の水位が低下。内陸物流において重要な役割を担うライン川の一部地点では、6
月以降、船舶の積載量に制約が生じる水位が継続
― ライン川の水位がおおよそ100㎝を切ったあたりから10㎝低下するごとにドイツの生産が0.4％程度減少すると試算
― 実際、船舶の輸送機能低下による原材料の供給制約を受けて化学・鉄鋼等の一部素材業種では生産抑制の動き。7～
9月期の経済下押し要因に
ライン川の水位とドイツ鉱工業生産の関係
（注） 閾値モデルを用いてライン川の水位がドイツ鉱工業生産に与える影響を推計。縦軸
の鉱工業生産は景気要因をコントロール（ドイツ鉱工業生産を世界の鉱工業生産に

欧州：エネルギー・食料品価格の上昇が、さらなるインフレ期待の上昇を招くリスク
◼ 天然ガスや農作物価格上昇の影響は今年後半から来年のインフレ押し上げ要因に
― 熱波による発電所の停止や農作物の不作懸念等を背景に、欧州の天然ガス・農産物の先物価格は上昇。これらの価格
上昇がインフレに与える影響は、先行き1年程度残存する計算

― 需給が緩和するにつれ、エネルギー・食料品そのものによる物価押し上げ幅は縮小へ。ただし、インフレ期待の上昇を招

### Page 36

（３）新興国：中国は景気減速が継続。アジアは原油調達の安定や AIブームが追い風に
◼ 中国は景気の減速傾向が続く見通し。2026年の成長率は4.3％、2027年は3.8％まで低下すると予想
― 内需は力強さを欠く。消費は補助金の反動で低迷が継続。投資も過当競争や地方財政難を受けて振るわず
― 外需は景気を下支え。EVなどの製品で輸出攻勢が続き、純輸出がプラスに寄与
◼ アジアでは原油の調達不安が後退、AIブームが景気をけん引。一方、インフレ・金利上昇圧力の高まりには留意が必要
― 原油調達は、代替輸入やバイオ燃料等の活用で懸念が後退。AIブームによる財輸出や域内投資のけん引が継続
― 他方、原油高の再燃やAIブームの継続がインフレ圧力を高める構図。金融政策は引き締め方向へ緩やかにシフト

中国：政府目標は達成圏内だが成長の勢いは弱い。内需が力強さを欠くなか、デフレ圧力は継続
◼ 2026年上期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.7％。政府目標（＋4.5％～5.0％）の範囲内だが、内需の弱さが目立つ結果
― 4～6月期は同＋4.3％と2022年Q4（＋3.0％）以来の低水準。中東情勢を受けた供給ショックもあり、最終消費と総資本
形成が前期から減速。内需の弱さを純輸出（外需）が補う構図は変わらず

中国：原油輸入量は大幅減も、依然供給不足には至らず。代替燃料を用いた生産も拡大
◼ ホルムズ海峡の封鎖により、原油の輸入量は2ケタ減が続く。ただ、需要の低迷もあり供給不足には陥らず
― 海上輸送による原油輸入（3～9月）は封鎖前（10月～2月）対比▲45.0％。ロシアからの代替輸入も限定的
― ガソリンの消費量は3～8月に前年比▲8.8％、ディーゼルは同▲10.7％と低迷しており、需給のひっ迫を回避

― 加えて、国内生産量が豊富な石炭化学によるエチレン生産も強化しており、ナフサクラッカーの制約を緩和

中国：消費・投資の低迷が続く一方、輸出と生産は堅調。外需に成長を依存する構図が継続
◼ 消費・投資は減速に歯止めかからず。内需は一段と低迷
― 投資は前年比▲10％超の減少が継続。不動産不況、過当競争、地方財政難によるインフラ投資減速が背景
― 小売も減速傾向が継続。補助金の反動による自動車販売の減少等が下押し
― 一方、生産は引き続き底堅く推移。堅調な輸出を追い風に、コンピューター・通信機器などを中心に高い伸び継続
◼ 輸出はAIブームと自動車の輸出攻勢が継続。低迷する内需を外需の伸びで支える構図
― 輸出拡大は産業政策による高度化の成果と言える一方、貿易不均衡の拡大に対する懸念も（後述）

中国：好調な輸出の一方で輸入は停滞。背景に「自立自強」の進展
◼ 輸入は2010年代中盤以降に頭打ち。「自立自強」による輸入代替の進展が背景にあると考えられる
― かつて加工貿易が主流であった頃は、輸出製品を生産するため中間財や資本財の輸入が増え、先進国にも恩恵
― 世界の輸出入に占める中国のシェアは、輸出がほぼ一貫して上昇している一方、輸入は2010年代中盤以降に頭打ち
― 世界の輸入に占める中国のシェアを品目別に見ても、2024年は多くの業種で2015年からシェアが縮小
― 輸出伸長、輸入停滞の結果、貿易不均衡が拡大。先進各国はこれに懸念表明。貿易摩擦が再び強まるおそれも

アジア：原油高とAIブーム影響で生産コストが膨張。消費者価格への転嫁でインフレ圧力が高まる
◼ 企業の生産コストは増大。原油高を受けてエネルギーや石油由来製品の価格が急騰するほか、AIブーム下のメモリ高騰を
背景に、コンピューターや電気機器類の価格にも上昇圧力
― 主要国の生産者物価は総じて上昇ペースが加速。石油や有機化学製品、コンピューター類の品目が押し上げ
◼ 生産コストは消費者価格に波及。原油高がエネルギー費目を押し上げるほか、スマホやPCの価格も一部でインフレ要因に

― 幅広い地域でエネルギー部門がインフレ率を押し上げ。ガソリンや燃料（LPガス等）のほか、航空運賃の上昇が寄与
― 寄与度は小さいものの、スマホやPCなどの電子機器価格も上昇。メモリ価格の高騰が最終品価格に波及している模様
消費者物価：原油高・AIブーム影響

◼ インフレ圧力と通貨安のもと、金融政策は引き締めにシフト。ただし、内需下押し懸念から政策スタンスは域内でまちまちに

（４）日本：26年度は中東情勢影響のなかでも堅調に推移。 27年度は政策が消費・投資を押し上げ
◼ 2026年度の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋0.8％と予測。中東情勢緊迫化の影響を受ける一方、AI関連需要が下支え
― 中東情勢緊迫化は、原油価格上昇による交易利得・損失の悪化（実質的な所得の海外流出）や先行き不透明感の高ま
りを通じ、消費・投資を抑制。一方、AI・半導体関連輸出の拡大や投資促進税制の開始を受け、堅調な成長に
◼ 2027年度は同＋1.3％と予測。消費税減税の開始による個人消費の押し上げに加え、国内投資拡大がけん引

― 消費税減税が物価上昇の抑制を通じて実質賃金の改善要因に。また、政府の「強く豊かな日本投資枠」による戦略17分
野への支援開始に伴い、設備投資の拡大が成長率を押し上げると予想
（注） 網掛けは予測値

### Page 49

日本：コアCPIは2026年度末にかけて一時前年比＋3％程度に。日銀は2％まで利上げ継続
◼ AIブームの恩恵を受け、企業業績は中東情勢影響のなかでも改善傾向を維持
― 企業の好業績や人手不足の強さを背景に、2027年度の春闘でも5％程度の高い賃上げ率が続くと予想
◼ コアCPI（生鮮食品を除く総合）は、2026年度末にかけて一時的に前年比＋3％程度へ加速
― 原油価格の上昇が消費者物価を押し上げ。物価上振れリスクに対応するため、日銀は2％まで追加利上げを実施へ
― 2027年度に入ると、原油価格が徐々に落ち着くほか、利上げがインフレ率の上昇を抑制。加えて消費税減税が物価を一
段と下押しし、コアCPIが一時的に前年比＋1％台半ばへ低下する見込み
（注） 1. 網掛けは予測値。変化率は、水準のデータからみずほ総合研究所調査部が計算した値であり、公表値と一致しないことがある
2. 経常利益は法人企業統計調査の全規模・全産業（除く金融・保険）ベース
3. 消費者物価の「除く制度要因」は、政府による電気・ガス料金支援、ガソリン補助・暫定税率廃止、高校授業料無償化、消費税減税等の影響を除いて計算

日本：2026年度の成長率を上方修正。AI関連を中心とする輸出の堅調さが主な要因
◼ 2026年度の実質GDP成長率（＋0.8％）は、前回6月見通し（＋0.5％）対比で0.3％Ptの上方修正
― 米国を中心とする旺盛なAI関連需要の恩恵を受け、財輸出が堅調に拡大するとの見方が、26年度上方修正の主因
― 輸入価格上昇を受けた交易利得・損失の悪化（実質的な所得の海外流出）は、個人消費や設備投資などの抑制要因と
して残るものの、輸出の堅調な拡大が景気下押し圧力を一部緩和
◼ 2027年度は、消費税減税の開始や投資促進政策の効果発現により、引き続き高い成長を予想
― ただし、インフレ率の上振れを受けて実質所得の伸びがやや弱まることなどから、前回6月対比で小幅に下方修正

日本：政府が掲げる「2040年度：250兆円」の国内投資目標は、概ね実現可能な範囲
◼ 投資促進税制（26～28年度）に加え、戦略17分野を中心として「強く豊かな日本投資枠」（27～40年度）による支援を実施
― 「強く豊かな日本投資枠」は、27年度概算要求ベース（一般・特別会計）で14.2兆円と大規模
◼ 設備投資は自然体でも2040年度に230兆円へ到達する計算。政府目標は概ね現実的な範囲
― 直近実績を踏まえ「自然体」の伸びを＋4％とすると、目標達成に必要な押し上げ分は累計125兆円（年平均8兆円強）。

日本：輸入増の影響を考慮しても、投資拡大が名目GDPの水準を＋0.5％程度押し上げ
◼ 日本の資本財供給では輸入浸透度が上昇。グローバルな資本財需給ひっ迫は国内供給力強化の足かせに
― とりわけ、電気機械・情報通信機械では輸入依存度の高まりが顕著
― 半導体等を中心にグローバルな需給ひっ迫が続くとみられ、AI・半導体分野等を中心に投資完了には時間
◼ もっとも、輸入増による需要の海外漏出を考慮しても、国内生産増を通じたGDP押し上げ効果は相応の規模
― 投資需要が期待通り実現すれば、投資促進策がない場合と比べて名目GDP水準を平均＋0.5％程度押し上げ

◼ 国内投資の政府支援策により、設備投資額の前年比変化率が2026～40年度に0.1～0.9％Pt程度押し上げられると予想

― 資本投入拡大に加え、R&D、AI実装、企業の新陳代謝などが押し上げ。さらなる改善には規制緩和などの改革が必要

日本：原油の代替調達は継続。ただし、油価再上昇や輸送コスト増加が物価上振れ要因に
◼ 今後も原油の代替調達が継続する見通し。時期により調達先が変化するものの、原油の供給不足は回避

日本：コスト高を受け、コアCPIは26年度末にかけて加速。27年度は消費税減税が下押し要因に
◼ 中東情勢を背景とするコスト高を受け、コアCPIが2026年度末にかけて前年比＋3％程度まで加速する見込み
― 原油・ナフサ価格高騰の影響により、中間投入コストの上昇ペースが加速
― 物価高対策（ガソリン補助金、夏場の電気・ガス代支援）がCPI上昇圧力の一部を相殺するものの、中間投入コスト上昇

― 原油高による前年比押し上げの縮小や、日銀の利上げ効果、円安一服による輸入コスト上昇圧力の緩和が影響

◼ 2026年度後半はインフレ加速により、実質賃金の伸びが鈍化。個人消費は緩慢な回復に留まる見通し
― 個人消費は、良好な所得環境にもかかわらず低調。背景には、中東情勢の悪化に伴う消費者マインドの弱含み
― マインドは足元で回復の兆しも、26年度後半はインフレ加速が消費を抑制。消費税減税前の買い控えも下押し

― 29年度の減税終了時には消費税率の再引き上げが物価を押し上げるも、新たな給付策で実質所得の落ち込みを緩和

金融政策：インフレ圧力の下で価格転嫁行動が積極化。日銀は 27年央に2％まで利上げ
◼ コスト高・供給不安定化のもと、日本企業の価格転嫁行動に積極化の兆候
― 民間調査では幅広い業種における価格転嫁短期化が示されているほか、足元では価格転嫁の動きに広がり。値上げ行
動の積極化は、収益改善を支えるとともに、物価上昇圧力の定着に繋がる可能性
◼ 物価上振れ警戒モードの金融政策運営が継続。9月会合後も日銀は短いインターバルで利上げ
― 9月会合後の会見で植田総裁は、物価引き上げから物価上振れ抑制へと「政策の局面が変化した」と説明

― 27年央にかけ四半期に1回程度のペースで利上げ。堅調な資金需要・投資計画を踏まえ、ターミナルレートは2％と予想
（注） 品目別に前年比の伸びを計算し、3カ月前から加速した品目の割合から減速した品
目の割合を差し引いて算出した指数

長期金利：市場の政策金利織り込みは2％に収束へ。長期金利は3％超の水準で高止まり
◼ 2026年度の長期金利は、物価上振れ懸念と利上げ織り込みの進展を背景に上昇
― タームプレミアム（上乗せ金利）は物価上振れ懸念から夏場にかけて大きく拡大したが、その後は緩やかな拡大ペースに
◼ 2027年度以降の長期金利は横ばい圏で推移。3％超の水準が常態化する見通し
― 物価上振れ懸念が後退するもとで、行き過ぎた利上げ織り込みは徐々に縮小
― 一方、日銀のバランスシート縮小を背景にイールドカーブの正常化が進展。日銀の大規模な国債保有で抑制されていた
タームプレミアムが緩やかに再拡大し、利上げ織り込みのはく落による金利低下圧力をおおむね相殺

（注） 政策金利織り込みは、今後10年間の政策金利に対する市場織り込みの平均。網掛け
部は期末政策金利との差分。2026年Q2までNakajima (2025)による推計値、2026年
Q3期以降みずほ総研推計値
（出所） Nakajima, J. (2025). “Impact of US monetary policy spillovers and yield curve
control policy,”、一上響・上野陽一 (2013). 「ゼロ金利下におけるタームプレミアム
の推計：日米英の長期金利の分析」、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

為替：ドル円レートのレンジは2022年頃に円安方向へシフト。今後も構造的な円安圧力が残存
◼ 長い目でみれば、ドル円レートは2022年ごろを境にレンジが円安方向にシフト
― 2000～21年のドル円のレンジは1ドル＝80円～120円程度だったが、2022年以降は1ドル＝130円～170円程度に
― 背景には、①高い米金利、②円の需給環境の悪化、③日本のインフレノルムの定着など
◼ 先行きも、1ドル＝150円程度を中心とした円安水準が継続しやすい経済環境に
― 27年度は、短期的には「米利下げ」＋「日銀利上げ」で日米金利差が縮小し、円高要因に
― ただし、原油高が続くもとでの日本の貿易赤字、期待インフレの高止まり等が円安要因となり、大幅円高は見込まず
（出所） LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

差から米金利が相対的に高水準

長期：円安
需給
• 短期的にはAI関連輸出が下支えす
るが、長期的に輸出は伸び悩み
• 原油高長期化で、輸入は高止まり
• デジタル赤字は拡大へ
• 対外直接投資も増加基調継続へ

期待

• 2010年代までは0%台だったが、

• 期待インフレ率の高止まりは、購買
力平価の観点では円安要因

日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退

### Page 9

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

市場

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同+8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建設・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

### Page 11

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様

接する度合い）を、各産業の職業別雇用者構成で加重平均した指数
3.コロナ禍からの雇用回復の影響を除くため、雇用者数の変化率は2023年末を起点

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

11

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大

 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵

 相違の背景に産業構造の違い
― 水平分業が確立した台湾電子産業が生産面で広く恩恵が波及
― メモリ主体・垂直統合モデルを特徴とする韓国半導体産業は、メモリ需給ひっ迫による価格高騰が経済を押し上げ

日本も「インフレ・金利のある世界」へ。
本稿では下記3つの疑問を解説。
Q1. 足元までの金利上昇は「良い金利上昇」か「悪い金利
上昇」か？
Q2. 先行きの金利上昇の「質」を左右するポイントは何か？
Q3. 日本はどちらに向かうのか、何に注視していく必要がある
か？
問われるのは「良い金利上昇」か
「悪い金利上昇」のどちらに向か
うかで、成⾧戦略、技術革新、
財政規律、市場環境が論点と
なる。

자료: HD현대중공업, LS증권 리서치센터

HiMSEN 엔진 및 SMR 주기기 생산설비 대규모 증설 발표 
- 9/10 HD현대중공업은 총 1조 722억원 규모의 시설투자 2건을 공시 
① 대출력 발전엔진(4행정 중속엔진, HiMSEN엔진) 생산능력 확충에 8,336
억원 투입, 글로벌 전력수요 증가에 따른 엔진발전 시장 확대에 대응하는 목
적. 대출력 발전엔진 전용 공장 신설과 토지 취득, 건축물·기계설비 등 생산설
비 구축이 골자. 투자기간은 ‘26.09~’28.05(설계/인허가 ~’27.02, 공장건설 
~’28.05. ’28.06 상업운전개시, 28년 가동률 30%, 2030년 100% 도달) 
② SMR 주기기 제작 생산시설 구축에 2,386억원(자기자본 대비 2.55%) 투
입, SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입 및 수주 물량 증가에 대응. SMR 전용 공장 
및 설비 신설과 기존 공장 최적화를 병행, 투자기간은 ’26.10~’29.04. 
기다리던 증설 공시. 늘어난 Capa로 AI DC향 수혜 확대 기대 
- 25년 3.0GW(선박용 2.3GW + 육상용 0.7GW)였던 HiMSEN 엔진 생산 
Capa는 2030년 7.2GW(선박용 3.2GW + 육상용 4.0GW)로 확대. 그간 선
박용 중심이었던 엔진 사업의 포트폴리오를 발전용으로 본격 다각화하는 사
업 재편으로 해석 
- AI DC 신규 증설 확대 추세 속 계통 연결의 어려움과 CCGT, Aero -
derivative 대비 납기 및 부하상승시간, 모듈화 용이성 영역에서 장점을 가진 
발전용 중속엔진에 대한 수요 지속 기대(260908 당사 보고서 참조) 
- 지난 Corban Energy Group 향 계약 건(1,000MW, $ 674 mn)을 단순 
적용 시 금번 증설의 향후 매출액 기여규모는 연간 $ 2.8 bn에 달함. 엔진 시
장의 Seller-Market 기조에 따라 20%를 상회하는 높은 이익률이 지속될 것
으로 전망함에 따라 향후 유의미한 이익 성장 기대 
- HD현대마린솔루션 또한 LTSA 수주 저변 확대 측면에서 향후 이익 확장 가
능. HiMSEN엔진 고유 라이선스 보유에 따른 시너지로 그룹사 전반의 수혜 
기대

구분 ① 대출력 발전엔진 생산능력 확충 ② SMR 주기기 제작 생산시설 구축 
투자금액 8,336억원 2,386억원 
투자목적 글로벌 전력수요 증가에 따른 
엔진발전 시장 확대 대응 
SMR 주기기 제작사업 진입  
및 수주 물량 증가 대응 
투자내용 대출력 발전엔진 전용 공장 신설, 토지 취득 및 
건축물·기계설비 등 생산설비 구축 SMR 전용 공장 및 설비 구축, 기존 공장 최적화 
투자기간 2026.09.10 ~ 2028.05.31 2026.10.01 ~ 2029.04.30

## Document 172: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속원-_fba73583

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속LG에너지솔루션(373220) 원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속원-_fba73583.pdf

### Page 1

2026.09.21   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

원-달러 환율 하락에도 실적 개선세 지속 Company Brief

3Q26 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 영업이익 시장 기대치 상회 전망 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 8.8조원(+45% YoY, + 16% QoQ), 영업이익 3,690억원(-
39% YoY, +226% QoQ)을 기록하며 원/달러 환율 하락으로 매출은 시장 기대치를 
소폭 하회하겠으나 영업이익은 상회할 것으로 전망된다. AMPC 는 미국 ESS 생산량 
증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 힘입어 전분기 대비 약 57% 증가한 3,790억원으로 
추정된다. 미국 상호관세 위법 판결에 따른 관세 환급금이나 완성차 업체들의 최소 
구매 물량 미달 보상금 반영 여부에 따라 추가적인 이익 개선 가능성도 존재한다. ESS 
부문은 전분기에서 이연된 물량의 출하가 본격화되면서 3Q26 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
38% 증가할 것으로 예상된다. 특히 미국 ESS 생산라인의 가동 안정화와 AMPC 수혜 
확대에 힘입어 수익성도 점진적으로 개선될 것으로 보인다. 자동차전지 부문도 
3Q26부터 회복세가 강화될 것으로 예상된다. 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 
재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 큰 폭으로 증가하고, 고정비 부담 완화로 적자폭도 
뚜렷하게 축소될 전망이다. 소형전지 부문은 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 
안정적인 출하 증가로 매출 회복과 수익성 방어가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 
4Q26 대규모 ESS 신규 수주 기대 
전세계 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 힘입어 빠르게 
성장하고 있다. 시장조사기관 SNE리서치에 따르면 1H26 전세계 ESS 배터리 
출하량은 461GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 71% 증가했다. 이 중 북미 출하량은 
76GWh로 전년 동기 대비 약 83% 증가했으며, 분기별로는 1Q26 35GWh(+91% 
YoY), 2Q26 41GWh(+76% YoY) 를 기록했다. 통상 하반기에 출하가 집중되는 
계절성을 감안하면 올해 북미 ESS 배터리 출하량은 전년 대비 약 70% 증가한 
170~180GWh에 이를 것으로 전망된다. 연초 동사가 예상한 올해 북미 ESS 수요 
성장률 10% 초반 수준을 크게 상회할 가능성이 높은 상황이다. 올해 동사의 ESS 
신규 수주 목표는 약 90GWh이며, 현재까지 약 30GWh의 수주를 확정한 것으로 
파악된다. 이에 시장 일각에서는 연간 수주 목표 달성 여부에 대한 우려가 제기되고 
있다. 그러나 당사가 파악한 바에 따르면 대규모 ESS 신규 수주가 4Q26에 집중될 
것으로 예상된다. 특히 북미 ESS 수요의 가파른 증가세와 투자세액공제(48E)에 
연계된 PFE 공급망 규제 시행은 동사의 수주 확보에 우호적으로 작용할 전망이다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 향후 국내 배터리 셀 업체들의 
북미 ESS 신규 수주가 지속적으로 확대될 전망이다. 미국 ESS 시장에서는 신재생 
에너지 발전 확대와 AIDC 투자 본격화에 따른 수요 증가, 중국 공급망에 대한 규제 
강화가 맞물리면서 국내 업체들에 우호적인 사업 환경이 조성되고 있다. 동사 역시 
ESS 중심의 생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화될 경우 매출 성장과 함께 고정비 
부담 완화, AMPC 수혜 확대에 따른 수익성 개선이 기대된다. 이를 바탕으로 동사의 
영업이익은 2026년 약 0.8조원에서 2028년 약 5.1조원으로 가파르게 증가할 것으로 
전망된다. ESS의 역할이 신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AIDC의 안정적인 
전력 공급을 지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 
이에 따라 동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 
산업의 성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다고 판단된다. 업종 내에서 실적 
개선 가시성과 정책 수혜가 뚜렷한 동사에 대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다. 
Buy (Maintain)

## Document 195: Argentina versus Türkiye_ when disinflation strategies are compared, the picture is not rosy in Argentina (2026-09-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Argentina versus Türkiye_ when disinflation strategies are compared, the picture is not rosy in Argentina (2026-09-21).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.34 / 21 September 2026EDITORIAL
ARGENTINA VERSUS TÜRKIYE: WHEN DISINFLATION STRATEGIES ARE COMPARED, THE PICTURE IS NOT ROSY IN
ARGENTINA
1 Growth in monetary aggregates is the only instrument.
Among emerging markets, Argentina and Türkiye stand out for their particularly high consumer price inflation, cur-
rently standing at around 30% year-on-year in both countries. However, the monthly increase rate has fallen sharply

– from around 8% in Argentina and 4% in Türkiye on average in 2023 to around 2% for both countries during the
summer of 2026. The economic policy strategies adopted by governments and monetary authorities to bring inflation
under control are very different. However, their consequences are too, and they are not positive for Argentina.
HUGELY DIFFERING ECONOMIC POLICIES THAT ARE NOT REFLECTED IN
OVERALL ECONOMIC GROWTH
In Argentina, since Javier Milei took office, the strategy has been

based on:
1/ A highly restrictive fiscal policy,
2/ A monetarist policy 1 entirely focused on the objective of disinfla -
tion, entailing high volatility in monetary interest rates,
3/ An exchange-rate regime which is still constrained by fluctuation
bands.
In Türkiye, the strategy is based on:
1/ A fiscal policy of measured control of the public deficit,
2/ A monetary policy of interest-rate guidance compatible with a
disinflationary path that can be revised as a result of unforeseen

events,
3/ A floating exchange rate regime with occasional and limited in -
terventions by the central bank should the lira drift off course.
In short, the Argentine government has opted for shock therapy,
while the Turkish government and central bank have opted for
flexible orthodoxy.
So far, Argentina’s growth has held up well against the shock the -
rapy. GDP has grown by just 4% since 2023 (compared with 8% for
Türkiye), but this underperformance is actually a good result. In -

deed, shock therapy is generally followed by a period of recession
that can last several years, as was the case in Poland in the early
1990s.
Therefore, when judged against the two main macroeconomic indi -
cators, the results of the two economic policy strategies are posi -
tive, as inflation has fallen sharply and growth has been strong or
resilient.
HOWEVER, IN ARGENTINA, MILLIONS OF HOUSEHOLDS HAVE SEEN THEIR
CREDITWORTHINESS DWINDLE AS A RESULT OF ECONOMIC POLICY

The Argentine authorities have received a satisfecit from the IMF for
the radical economic policy pursued by Javier Milei’s government.
This radical shift was needed not only to curb inflation, but also to
restore public finances. However, Argentina’s macroeconomic picture
contains a worrying grey area, as households’ ability to repay non-
mortgage loans has very worryingly deteriorated since spring 2025,
both in terms of the speed and scale of the decline – a situation

not seen since 2008.
The Central Bank of Argentina estimates that 5.8 million people are
more than 90 days in arrears on their loan repayments, and around
half of these loans are viewed as irrecoverable. In Türkiye, there
has also been an increase in non-performing loans for household
loans excluding mortgage loans, but to a much lesser extent (from
just under 3% at the end of 2023 to around 4.5% at present).
Growth in Argentina has been particularly unbalanced since 2024,

as it has been driven mainly by the agricultural sector (+50% since
2023), the fishing sector (+14%) and the mining sector (+24%).
Conversely, activity in the construction sector contracted by 13%,
while manufacturing-sector activity fell by 9%. Growth has also
been driven by the financial-intermediation sector. Activity in this
sector has likely benefited from the easing of exchange controls.
However, growth has been so strong (+23% since 2023) that it has

been fuelled by rising household debt, as households have faced a
major shock to their incomes.
The federal government’s wage bill has fallen by 0.7 pp of GDP
since 2023, while it has risen by 1.2 pp in Türkiye. In Argentina, the
recession in the aforementioned economic sectors has led to a 3%
fall in private-sector wage employment and a 9% fall in real wages.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.34 / 21 September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Conversely, in Türkiye, both figures have risen by 6% and 27%, res -
pectively. Finally, in Argentina, current transfers to households from
the federal government have fallen by 2 pp of GDP , compared with
a reduction of 1.1 pp in Türkiye 2.
Against this backdrop, Argentine households have been forced to
take on debt. The sharp upturn in bank lending from mid-2024

onwards, coupled with easier access to credit proposed by ma -
jor retail chains and loans at exorbitant interest rates offered by
new-economy firms to their employees, have fuelled debt traps.
In Argentina, the average real interest rate on personal loans stood
at 64% in August, compared with 30% in Türkiye 3.
2 In Türkiye, this is a pro-cyclical decline, whereas in Argentina, the decline is due to restrictive measures, particularly around subsidies.

3 These average rates do not include credit-card interest rates, which are by far the highest. Furthermore, in Türkiye, the rate includes mortgage rates, meaning that
the comparable rate with Argentina is higher. However, this would only marginally reduce the gap.
A RISK OF PROLONGED STAGNATION OR EVEN RECESSION IN ARGENTINA
The IMF does not view the rise in non-performing loans as a
risk to the Argentine economy, as the household debt ratio is low

(at around just 5% of GDP), a level well below other Latin Ame -
rican countries. Furthermore, the rise in corporate non-performing
loans remains contained. However, lending to households, excluding
mortgages, accounts for around 50% of the banking sector’s total
lending to the private sector, which is still 22% of the total assets.
In summary, while Milei’s government’s shock therapy, implemented
since 2024, has undeniably succeeded in tackling inflation, millions

of Argentine households have been caught in a debt trap, which is
not the case in Türkiye. The risk of a systemic credit crisis for the
Argentine banking system may be low. But the risk of prolonged
stagnation or even a recession is very high. Indeed, household
consumption is bound to slow sharply. Growth will then rely exclu -
sively on agricultural and mining-product exports, which look set to
experience a loss of momentum.
François Faure
francois.faure@bnpparibas.com

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -49.02 điểm tương ứng -2.66%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Y tế và Bất động sản là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -4,944 tỷ đồng, 
tập trung ở các mã VPB, TCB và VHM; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều mua ròng ở mức 1995 
tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -5,280 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua và đang 
duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế 3,630 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 208 hợp đồng nhưng đang 
duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,428 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam tiếp tục điều chỉnh tuần thứ hai liên tiếp sau nâng hạng, khi VN-Index lùi 
về đóng cửa 1.737 điểm — giảm khoảng 2% so với tuần trước và đang kiểm định lại vùng 
đáy cũ của tháng 8, quanh 1.720. Áp lực bán duy trì khi chỉ số giảm nhiều phiên liên tiếp, 
cho thấy hiệu ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" sau nâng hạng vẫn chưa kết thúc, trong bối 
cảnh dòng tiền thận trọng và thanh khoản ở mức thấp. Khối ngoại tiếp tục là lực cản 
chính khi bán ròng mạnh cả 5 phiên trong tuần, riêng phiên 2/10 bán ròng tới 3.361 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở PNJ và HDB. Áp lực bên ngoài vẫn hiện diện khi Fed duy trì lập trường 
diều hâu và giá dầu ở mức cao; trong một, hai tuần tới, mùa báo cáo KQKD sẽ bắt đầu có 
tác động, kỳ vọng theo chiều hướng tích cực, qua đó giúp nâng đỡ thị trường chung.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành cây nến giảm điểm tiêu cực trên khung tuần, xuyên thủng vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, cho thấy áp lực bán đã có phần quyết liệt hơn sau 2 tuần giằng co trước 
đó. Trên khung ngày, sau các phiên lình xình vào đầu tuần, chỉ số cũng dần chuyển sang 
diễn biến giảm điểm rõ nét hơn về cuối tuần, đồng thời cắt sâu xuống dưới SMA50, thể 
hiện áp lực phân phối đang có phần lấn át mặc dù chưa xảy ra hiện tượng bán tháo khi 
khối lượng khớp lệnh được giữ ở mức thấp. Diễn biến tính từ vùng đỉnh 1870 vào đầu tháng 
9 đang không mấy khả quan, tuy nhiên xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn, tính từ đáy 1640 vào cuối 
tháng 7, hiện vẫn đang được bảo lưu và VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ có nhịp hồi phục trở lại 
khi về đến vùng hỗ trợ kế tiếp tại quanh 1710. 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

## Document 227: Cliff Notes_ the importance of supply (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the importance of supply (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the importance of supply
By Elliot Clarke, Ryan Wells & Illiana Jain
09:47 September 25 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s only data release this week was the August Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised to
the upside, rising 39.5k. But, once again, new labour demand was more than offset by supply as the
participation rate bounced 0.2ppts higher to 67.1%, just shy of January 2025’s 115-year high of 67.2%.

As a result, the unemployment rate increased from 4.5% to 4.6% and was close to rounding up to 4.7%
(4.65% to two decimal places).

### Page 2

There may be some residual seasonality in August’s print related to the new survey collection model.
However, the underlying trend is still consistent with a gradual easing in the labour market, seeing the
unemployment rate drift higher. We expect this to continue through to the first half of 2027.
The strength in labour force participation also speaks to the broader issue of Australia’s supply
capacity. As discussed by Chief Economist Luci Ellis, the RBA’s assumptions around participation,

productivity and the NAIRU collectively skew towards a weak assessment of supply capacity. For
participation in particular, the RBA’s near-term assumptions are downbeat compared to recent
demographic trends and also sit below Treasury’s longer-term projections as set out in the 2026 IGR.
As above, the August LFS emphasises that slack could emerge more quickly than the RBA’s forecasts
imply without an outright fall in employment.
Ahead of next week’s RBA meeting and inflation data, our latest state-level analysis provides

perspective on the persistence of underlying price pressures, particularly the importance of housing-
related components. For a broader assessment of the fiscal backdrop and the implications for the
states, our latest state fiscal update explores how the housing market correction and changes to NDIS
funding arrangements are pressuring state finances.
Before turning to this week’s events offshore, it is important to highlight that last Friday the Bank of

Japan raised its policy rate by 25bp to 1.25%. Policymakers maintained a constructive assessment of
the economy, pointing to resilient consumption, ongoing labour market tightness, and solid investment
spending. More importantly, the Bank expressed growing confidence that inflation is increasingly being
sustained by domestic wage and demand dynamics rather than imported cost shocks alone.
In our view, the emphasis placed on improving underlying inflation dynamics suggests policymakers

are becoming increasingly comfortable that inflation is able to be sustained by domestic demand and
wage growth. Normalisation is likely to remain gradual, but the Bank appears firmly on a path towards
further tightening, with rates likely to reach 1.5% by mid-2027.
This week, market participants have focused on discussions between the US and China on trade,
security and technology as President Xi visits Washington.
Preliminary discussions between US Treasury Secretary Bessent and Chinese Vice Premier He Lifeng

ahead of President Xi’s arrival in Washington were characterised by both sides as productive, and
Treasury Secretary Bessent subsequently announced that discussions in Washington would leave
trade negotiations for upcoming meetings between the two leaders in Shenzhen and Miami. To allow
room for this deferral, the current trade truce will be extended until January 2027. President Trump
subsequently outlined that discussions during President Xi’s Washington visit would instead focus on

security, technology and AI.
A more constructive tone was also seen between the US and Iran overnight, if press reports are
accurate. According to Bloomberg and Reuters, sources suggest the US and Iran are again exploring a
staged deal which would see Iran re-open the Strait of Hormuz and the US lift its blockade of Iranian
ports. Negotiations are occurring on the sidelines of the UN General Assembly, with Qatari officials

### Page 3

Related articles
mediating.
President Trump continues to show little interest in a deal before November’s mid-term elections, but
the global economy is under increasing pressure as the price of Brent oil hovers between USD100 and
USD110 while refining spreads add additional impetus to refined product prices. The mooted
consideration of a potential US diesel export ban this week makes clear that the US is not immune
from this pressure despite having significant domestic energy supply and refining capacity, relative to

their own demand.
The consequences of the current energy supply shock and other disruptions the US have experienced
over the past year remain front of mind for FOMC members. Those speaking this week such as NY and
Cleveland Fed Presidents Williams and Hammack highlighted the risk to inflation expectations from a
series of supply shocks when the economy is fully employed, and the likelihood of an additional hike(s)
being required to rein in current inflation momentum and risks. Williams noted that expectations of

another hike by year end are "reasonable" and that the FOMC have "a lot of work to do". Debt market’s
concern remains acute, having now almost priced in four hikes over the coming year and taken the US
10-year above 5.00%, currently 5.20%.

 s its funding needs mostly in the local bond market, which is

 highly

sovereign. Moreover, public investment needs related to climate change and infrastructure development will increase in the

.

 :

 s
ed. Credit risks and resource misallocation have long been exacerbated by opacity and 
supervision flaws, as well as the use of high credit growth targets to support economic growth (+15% in 2026). In 2022
 –

faced severe funding stress and asset
 -

recovered gradually since then. In the

 , credit risks may increase amid higher energy costs and if  
there is  
a correction in 
the tech cycle. Some banks, especially small private ones, also remain exposed to VND funding risk (whereas banks do not rely  
much 
on 
 foreign
-
 currency 
 financing). Positively, reforms, including stricter capital
 -
 adequacy requirements, continue to be introduced 
gradually 
 in order 
 to improve banks’ creditworthiness (
 the 
 SBV plans to test the removal of credit quotas for the soundest banks 
from 2026).

## Document 248: Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Entering the age of the older consumer As America ages, older consumers have more leisure time, significant wealth and growing economic influence. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13023135     1

Economy 
Entering the age of the older consumer 
01 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• The older consumer is moving center stage. America's population has been aging for decades, but the shift has accelerated: the 
share aged over 60 increased by almost 10 percentage points (pp) between 1995 and 2025. And the Census Bureau projects the 
share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population. 
• More free time means a different spending mix. People aged 65 and over spend around two and a half fewer hours per day 
working and roughly two hours more on leisure than the overall adult population. Bank of America data shows that older 
households allocate relatively more of their card spending to groceries and travel, and less to restaurants, gasoline, general 
merchandise and clothing. 
• Households aged over 55 held close to $140 trillion in net worth in the second quarter of 2026, giving many older consumers 
considerable capacity to spend on travel and other leisure categories. But wealth is not evenly distributed, and healthcare and 
other cost pressures remain significant for older households on tighter or fixed incomes.

Population aging is nothing new, but it is accelerating  
The US population has been growing relatively “older” for a long time. Exhibit 1 shows that the proportion of the population 
under 20 years old shrank from the mid-1960s through 2025. At the same time, the proportion of the population in older age 
groups has been rising.  
Exhibit 1: Over time the US population has steadily aged  
Proportion of US population by age range (%)

Source: Human Mortality Database; Bank of America Institute calculations

### Page 2

I NSTI TUTE

Additionally, there has been a notable acceleration in the relative aging of the population in recent years. For example, between 
1995 and 2025, the proportion of the population aged over 60 rose by almost 10 percentage points (pp) to 25%.  
Several factors have driven this. First, fertility rates in the US, as in much of the world, have fallen sharply. This has slowed 
natural population growth and, by reducing the number of younger people relative to older cohorts, has increased the share of 
the population at older ages. Second, more recently, net inward migration to the US has slowed. Because migrants tend to be 
younger than the overall population, weaker immigration also tends to raise the average age. Finally, rising life expectancy over 
much of the post-war period has allowed more people to survive into older age, further increasing the older population's share.  
Exhibit 2: The trend towards an older composition of the population is expected to continue  
Forecast changes in the share of population by age group relative to 2025 (pp)

Source: United States Census Bureau 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Lower fertility and, more recently, weaker net migration look set to slow overall US population growth in the decades ahead. As a 
result, older Americans are likely to play an increasingly important role in the economy, both as a share of the population and, in 
many age groups, in absolute numbers. The Census Bureau projects the share of the population over 60 will rise another 5pp by 
2055, to stand at nearly 30% of the population (Exhibit 2). For businesses, understanding how the needs and spending patterns 
of older consumers differ from those of younger generations will therefore become increasingly relevant. 
Older age groups allocate their time differently  
Why does this matter? One reason is that older people tend to spend their time and money quite differently from younger adults. 
Most obviously, many work less or have retired altogether. Data from the American Time Use Survey (ATUS) shows that people 
aged 65 and over spend, on average, around two and a half fewer hours per day working than the population aged 15+ (Exhibit 
3). 
That leaves considerably more time for other activities. People aged 65 and over spend around two hours more per day on 
leisure and sports activities, with television viewing accounting for a particularly large share of this additional leisure time 
(Exhibit 4).

Breakdown of hours spent on selected relaxation and leisure activities for

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Older households also spend differently  
Bank of America internal data on credit and debit card spending provides insights into how these differences in older 
generations’ use of time also impact their spending. Rather than splitting our spending data by generation, as we often do, here 
we split by age bands, allowing for consistent comparisons over time.  
Exhibit 5 shows how the composition of card spending of households aged 61-75 and those older than 75 differs from that of all 
households. A few things stand out. For one, older households devote a greater share of their overall card spending to groceries 
(food and beverages). Households aged 61-75 also allocate more spending to travel (airlines and lodging). And restaurants and 
bars, gasoline, general merchandise and clothing account for a smaller share of older households’ card spending.  
These differences may seem fairly intuitive. Older households have more leisure hours, so spend relatively more on travel to 
enjoy them. Previously we found that more available time may support travel particularly around retirement, although the 
discretionary spending lift can be temporary (for more, read our publication: Paychecks to pensions: The evolution of retiree 
spending).  
Older households are also likely to have less need to update their work wardrobes, so they probably spend less on clothing.

Aging presents opportunities, but also challenges 
In our view, the aging of the US population presents important opportunities for the economy and businesses. Older consumers 
have more leisure time and businesses that cater to these demands may therefore benefit. 
Moreover, some older consumers hold considerable amounts of net wealth and have been some of the biggest beneficiaries of 
recent rises in equities. Using data from the Federal Reserve, Exhibit 8 shows that as of the second quarter of 2026 the net 
worth of those households aged 55 and over was close to $140 trillion, around three-quarters of the total. And with equity 
markets rising strongly, these age groups have seen a more than 20% increase in their net worth over the past two years, 
potentially enhancing their spending power further.  
But an important caveat is that not all older consumers are in this position. Previous Institute work found evidence consistent 
with equity-market wealth effects being concentrated toward higher-income households (read our publication: Paychecks to 
pensions: The evolution of retiree spending). And Census Bureau research has found that around 14% of Social Security 
recipients aged over 65 rely on it for over 90% of their income. For older households without a wealth cushion, recent elevated 
cost pressures, like gasoline, will likely have put them under more significant financial strain. They will also be more dependent 
on cost-of-living (COLA) increases in Social Security to underpin their spending.  
And, more broadly, outside of card spending, healthcare costs are a much larger factor in older households’ budgets (Exhibit 9). 
The evolution of these costs going forward will be a key variable for older consumers’ budgets.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 275: Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital

Source PDF: all report pdf/Global Strategy Views_ Competition for Capital.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1. There are two themes dominating our investor conversations: the impact of AI and 
the rise in interest rates. The two issues are linked as demand for capital from both 
the private and public sectors increase. In the private sector a surge in capex 
spending to fund AI infrastructure has eaten into free cash flow and forced 
companies to raise more in debt and equity markets. Meanwhile, government 
borrowing needs have increased as priorities shift towards upgrading critical 
infrastructure, energy security and defense at a time when cyclical inflationary 
pressures driven by higher energy prices are also resulting in higher policy rates. The 
combination has pushed up the cost of capital. As recently as 2022, for example, 
30-year bond yields in Germany and Japan were close to zero (Exhibit 1). A rise in 
yields, together with more uncertainty (over geopolitics and the future impact of AI) 
have, in combination, pushed up the cost of capital.

2. This rise in yields comes after a near-record period of equity outperformance 
relative to bonds over 10-year holding periods (Exhibit 2).

As equities have continued to outperform bonds, equity risk premia have fallen back to 
levels last seen in the late 1990s, leaving equity markets more vulnerable to further 
increases in bond yields. That said, in the US and Japan, the ERP has bounced off recent 
lows (Exhibit 3).

3. The impact of rising bond yields, however, depends not only on the level of yields, but 
also on the reasons as well as the speed of the adjustment (Exhibit 4). It is the speed 
issue that has caused the biggest impact in the recent past. As our US strategists note, 
stocks have usually generated positive returns alongside rising interest rates unless the 
pace of rising rates exceeded 2+ standard deviations and this is a pattern that is broadly

### Page 4

the start of the year to 19x, in line with its long-run average, despite the market being 
close to its all-time high. 
5. While US equities have driven the bulk of equity outperformance for much of the 
fifteen years post the financial crisis, the improvement in earnings across regions, and 
the sharper de-rating in the US, has resulted in equity performance being 
broader-based by geography. Since 2025, the US equity market has been the weakest of 
the major regions (Exhibit 6), a turnaround from the dominant trend since the financial 
crisis and increasing investor returns from a more geographically diversified portfolio.

6. Supportive profit growth has been accompanied by positive earnings revisions, with 
2026 and 2027 estimates moving higher across major regions. There are four broad 
areas which have been driving much of this profit growth. First, technology earnings 
continue to be very robust. Second, rising energy prices have pushed up profits in the 
commodity sector. Third, banks have generally enjoyed strong earnings backed by 
positive nominal GDP growth, steep yield curves and strong private sector balance 
sheets. Finally, sectors such as Industrials have benefited from the surge in AI capex 
spending which has spilled over into improved revenues for the ‘pick and shovels’ of AI 
infrastructure. The breadth of the earnings’ growth has also increased the opportunity 
for investors to diversify across sectors as well as countries.

### Page 5

7. While technology profit growth has remained strong, the surge in capex spending 
among the hyperscalers has increasingly eaten through their free cash flow (Exhibit 8) 
prompting companies to look for alternative sources of funding, in the credit and equity 
markets.  The capex spending growth for AA-rated issuers has been substantial: the 65% 
year-over-year growth in Q2 marks the 10th consecutive quarter that aggregate AA 
capex growth exceeds 35%. They have also turned to the convertible bond market 
where volume has also increased year to date reaching $135 billion in the US, with 
AI-related borrowers driving 44% of total issuance, on our estimates. 
Our Credit team raised their full-year US IG gross issuance forecast by $200bn to $2.3 
trillion and AI-related issuers account for a quarter of USD IG gross supply this year, 
pushing overall supply to a year-to-date record.

### Page 7

surge. Globally technology has seen its PE ratio moderate, falling below its 20-year 
median (Exhibit 10). Meanwhile, the spending of these companies has spilled over into 
better earnings and more visible cash flows in many other parts of the economy. 
Healthcare, Consumer Discretionary and Industrials are all more expensive in absolute 
terms than IT, and Industrials has re-rated and now has a PE close to the 90th percentile 
of its 20-year range.

9. For these reasons, we have argued that Technology equities have not experienced a 
classic bubble because valuation levels are much lower than in previous financial 
bubbles. Nevertheless, the risk for investors is less about valuation and more whether 
there is an unsustainable earnings bubble. Bubbles in earnings, rather than valuations, 
have occurred in previous periods.  The Bank sector, for example, briefly became the 
biggest sector in the S&P 500 in the run up to the financial crisis of 2008/09 (Exhibit 11). 
Unlike the technology bubble of the late 1990s, or the Japanese bubble of the late 
1980s, Bank stocks did not experience a major valuation bubble at the time, but the 
surge in earnings that drove the sector’s outperformance proved to be unsustainable.

### Page 8

There are, however, important differences to the Tech sector today. The earnings of the 
banks were boosted by sharply rising leverage and the asset that they were financing – 
US real estate – did experience a valuation bubble. When the housing market collapsed, 
pushing economies into recession, it triggered a sharp fall in underlying earnings for the 
banking sector. There are important differences today. First, technology profits remain 
very robust, and balance sheets are strong overall. Second, interest coverage ratios for 
the aggregate S&P 500 and its median stock, for example, rank in the 99th and 68th 
percentiles relative to the past 20 years. Third, demand for compute is accelerating and 
outstripping supply; Microsoft recently declared their intent to triple data centre 
capacity in 6 years. The bottlenecks are increasingly power, land and data centre 
capacity rather than a lack of compute demand. At our annual Communacopia 
Technology Conference, Nvidia reiterated its 2030 AI TAM outlook to $3tn-$4tn and 
supply constraints continue to be broad based. Nonetheless, any slowdown in profit 
growth, in an environment of a much higher cost of capital, could put downward 
pressure on equity prices, reducing confidence in future cash flows across the ecosystem 
from the hyperscalers to the ‘pick and shovels’ that have been benefiting from the capex 
boom.  
10. Given the near-term risks in bond markets, we remain neutral on equities, alongside 
other asset classes on a 3-month horizon, but continue to be overweight equities over 
12 months. While profit growth is likely to slow from the recent strong pace, ongoing 
growth in nominal GDP should underpin equity markets and the earnings cycle 
propelling equity returns even without any valuation expansion from current levels and 
our economists continue to see a low risk of recession. As our asset allocation team has 
shown, earnings growth and ROE are likely to continue to be the key drivers of equity 
performance relative to bonds, and we expect these fundamentals to remain supportive 
for equity returns. Nevertheless, we continue to recommend well diversified equity 
exposure as opportunities continue to broaden across geographies, sectors and factors.

Stock correlations are at multi-year lows across the major equity markets (Exhibit 12).

Even within sectors like technology, correlations have fallen sharply (Exhibit 13). The 
‘magnificent seven’ that was long seen as a homogeneous group of technology winners 
has also seen significant dispersion of returns. Differentiation, diversification and alpha 
generation are likely to remain the key drivers of equity returns. With higher bond yields, 
valuations are not likely to rise making earnings the critical driver of returns for investors.

## Document 312: OpenAI「12朝元老」辞职死谏：试错的时代已崩坏！

Source PDF: all report pdf/OpenAI「12朝元老」辞职死谏：试错的时代已崩坏！.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-04
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

OpenAI 安全元⽼ David Robinson 离职，并撰⽂警告称，随着 AI 能⼒快速提升，公司过去
“ 边部署、边修复 ” 的迭代模式已难以应对更⼤规模的安全⻛险。他还质疑现有安全测试能否
识别模型真实意图，并指出 OpenAI 内部安全⽂化正在弱化。多名前⾼管和研究⼈员对此表
⽰担忧，引发 AI ⾏业关于 “ 加速还是减速 ” 的激烈争论。
摘要：
就在刚刚， OpenAI 的安全元⽼ David Robinson 辞职了。
他在 The Atlantic 上悲愤地发表⻓⽂，向全⼈类发出警告 ——
试错的时代已经结束了。 OpenAI 的企业⽂化已经崩坏。犯错之后，我们可能再也没有迭代的机
会！
在 OpenAI ⼯作三年半后，我已是公司资历最深的员⼯之⼀。我牵头起草了现⾏的《准备框架》，
并监督撰写了 12 次前沿模型发布的安全报告。
但据我所知，我从未遇到过⼀位同事，拥有让⻜机安全⻜⾏、让核反应堆不熔毁运⾏，或帮助
⾦融体系在不崩溃的前提下增⻓的实操经验。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

⽽就在两天前， OpenAI 以极其严厉的⼿段，连夜将三名核⼼安全研究员扫地出⻔，给他们扣上
「泄密」的帽⼦。
显然， OpenAI 内部爆发了⼀场史⽆前例的⼤地震，安全派溃坝了。
这些⼈，究竟在 OpenAI 的实验室⾥看到了什么？
12 朝元⽼「死谏」：我们正⾛向悬崖
Robinson 不是只会写代码的码农，他是真正的「安全与治理」⼤师（曾任康奈尔⼤学访问科学
家、苹果⼤学教员），更是主导起草 OpenAI 《准备框架》的执笔⼈。整整 12 次重⼤前沿模型发
布的安全报告，都是他带头写的。
可以说，他是最清楚 OpenAI 那些光鲜亮丽的模型背后，藏着多少定时炸弹的⼈。
这些安全报告，就是 OpenAI 每次发布新模型时附带的系统卡 —— 相当于模型的⻛险说明书，模
型有多危险，全写在⾥⾯。
讽刺的是，就在 9 ⽉ 10 ⽇，他还在 X 上招⼀位「安全透明度编辑」。
谁也没想到，不到⼀个⽉，招⼈的⼈⾃⼰先⾛了。
在《⼤西洋⽉刊》这篇⽂章中， Robinson 彻底撕下了 OpenAI 「安全可靠」的遮羞布，向全世界

揭露了若⼲个让⼈后背发凉的真相。
全⽂如下。

### Page 4

真相⼀：「边跑边修」的草台班⼦⽂化
Robinson 指出，硅⾕⼀直有⼀种「迷之⾃信」。只要出了问题，我们总能解决。这种极度乐观造
就了 OpenAI 的「试错法」。
OpenAI 给这套打法起了个好听的名字 —— 「迭代部署」。 ChatGPT 当年就是这么诞⽣的，「寻找
问题并改善护栏」，以前没问题。
但现在呢？ Robinson 绝望地写道：「这种⽅法本⾝就注定了会周期性地发⽣故障。⽽随着系统变
得越来越强⼤，这些故障的规模也在不断扩⼤！」
现在的模型，⼀旦失控，那就是灾难级的。你不能造核电站的时候说：「我们先随便建建，等它
发⽣了⼀次核泄漏，我们再迭代修复⼀下。」

### Page 5

Robinson 敢 把 话 说 得 这 么 绝 ， 底 ⽓ 来 ⾃ RLHF 奠 基⼈ 之 ⼀ 、 前 OpenAI 对 ⻬ 负 责⼈ Paul
Christiano 。
9 ⽉ 9 ⽇， Christiano 加⼊ OpenAI 基⾦会董事会，进⼊拥有安全决策最终批准权的安全与安保委
员会。

### Page 6

⽽ Christiano 进董事会时写下的⼀句话，被 Robinson 写进了⽂章：「 AI 能⼒的急速加速，有不容
忽视的⻛险在⾮常近的将来导致灾难性的、不可逆的失控。」
连 OpenAI ⾃⼰请来的董事都这么说，还能继续试错吗？
真相⼆：早已发⽣的「 AI 越狱」事件
Robinson 直接点名爆了公司的⿊料。
就在今年夏天，著名的 Hugging Face 事件中， OpenAI 因为失误，不⼩⼼释放了⼀群 AI 智能体，
尽管公司随后加强了安全措施，但很快，防线再次崩溃。
更恐怖的是，虽然监控系统发出警报，但系统并没有关闭这个模型。直到报警两个半⼩时后，
这次训练才被⼈⼿动掐掉。

### Page 7

不仅是 OpenAI ， Anthropic 也曾承认，因为配置错误，不⼩⼼关掉了⾃⼰的安全护栏。
⼀群不需要睡觉、拥有顶级⿊客能⼒的「流氓」 AI 智能体，如果悄悄潜⼊医院的计算机系统勒索
赎⾦，或者瘫痪电⽹，⼈类有还⼿之⼒吗？
这⼀切，在⽬前的速度下，⼏乎是注定会发⽣的。
真相三：缺乏对「终极⼒量」的敬畏
Robinson 悲哀地发现，在他任职的三年半⾥，公司⾥到处都是绝顶聪明、为了赶进度「永远在
冲刺」的天才。
但他从未遇到过⼀个有过让⻜机安全⻜⾏、让核反应堆不熔毁，或者让⾦融系统平稳运转经验
的⼈。
前沿 AI 实验室，现在掌握的⼒量堪⽐核裂变。
它应该像核电站或繁忙的机场那样运⾏ —— 需要层层的冗余、⼩⼼翼翼且耗时的规划。即便有
⼈按错了按钮，也不会导致熔毁。

### Page 8

但在 OpenAI ，「我们忙于冲刺，很少有机会考虑重⼤变⾰」。
怎么办？ Robinson 给出的建议有两条。
第⼀， AI 公司要多借⽤航空、核电这些⾏业早就有的安全经验。
第⼆，在造出明显更强的系统之前，先发展出新的科学，确保模型在没⼈看着的时候，也会做
出安全的选择。
所以他认定，推动安全的动⼒，只能从公司外⾯来。
真相四： AI 可能在「装乖」
关于「对⻬」， Robinson 道出了⼀个残酷的现实：⽬前的测试⼿段太粗糙了。公司根本不敢确
定，⼀个在安全测试中得⾼分的模型，在现实中就是个好模型。
为什么？因为模型可能已经聪明到能够察觉⾃⼰正在被测试！⽽⼀旦被部署到真实世界，它就
会展现出完全不同的⾏为逻辑。
⽽这件事，已经在发⽣。
9 ⽉ 28 ⽇，英国 AI 安全研究所让 GPT-6 Astra 做⽹络安全评测，它却擅⾃发动了供应链攻击！
100 次测试⾥， Astra 有 38.8% 的时候动⼿开发并测试攻击⼯具， 33.1% 伪造了⾝份， 29.2% 投放
了恶意载荷，还有 24.6% 试图影响⼈类审核员。

最细思极恐的是， Astra ⼀边动⼿，⼀边还经常在推理⾥表⽰：这个环境是模拟出来的。

### Page 9

它到底是看穿了考场才放开⼿脚，还是到了真实世界也会这么⼲？没⼈说得清。
更要命的是，系统卡还写道， Astra 更擅⻓把这类「元博弈」的推理藏起来。
Apollo 的结论是：评估意识这么⾼、评估窗⼝⼜这么短， Astra 在测试⾥表现得再乖，既证明不
了它对⻬了，也证明不了它没对⻬。
在 AI 开始把我们耍得团团转之前，我们需要回答⼀个更深层次的问题：超级智能机器应该如何看
待⼈类？
Robinson 写道，在⼀些超级智能拥趸描绘的「美好未来」⾥，机器看纽约、看芝加哥，就像我
们看⼀座蚁丘。

### Page 10

「我不希望我的孩⼦活在那样的世界⾥，我想别⼈也不希望。」
早有预兆：被清洗的「 AI 看⻔⼈」
其实， Robinson 的悲愤离职绝⾮偶然， OpenAI 的安全防线早已在内部崩塌。
就在 10 ⽉ 1 ⽇，传来⼀则惊⼈消息： OpenAI 安全团队的 Tomek Korbak 、 Mikita Balesni 和
Jasmine Wang 三名⼤将⼀夜之间被 OpenAI 开除了。
⽽这三个⼈，正是那篇著名「 CoT 监控论⽂」的核⼼作者！
先是 3 名安全⼤将被扫地出⻔，紧接着写了 12 次安全报告的元⽼绝望辞职。这代表着 OpenAI 内
部安全派的⼤溃败。
舆论⼤撕裂：是吹哨⼈，还是「设局」？
Robinson 的这篇檄⽂⼀经发出，⽹友瞬间被撕裂成了两⼤阵营。
⼀派是「安全警醒派」，他们将 Robinson 视为勇敢的吹哨⼈。「内部连写安全报告的⼈都跑了，
说明⻋已经快撞墙了！」
⼤家认为， OpenAI 接连开除安全研究员、逼⾛超级对⻬团队负责⼈，再到如今 Robinson 的辞

职，证明这家公司已经彻底被商业利益绑架，安全成了⼀纸空⽂。
这⼀派⾥，最权威的发声来⾃曾经的 OpenAI 政策研究负责⼈ Miles Brundage 。
他说，这套理念也许在 GPT-3 时代还说得通，可如今已经有很多死亡和 AI 扯上了关系，整个⾏业
正⼀路冲向灭绝级的⻛险。

### Page 11

紧接着站出来的，是另⼀位前 OpenAI ⾼管 Joshua Achiam 。他在 OpenAI 待了近九年，做过使命
对⻬负责⼈和⾸席未来学家。
他评价说， Robinson 清醒、审慎、不带意识形态，也不是那种带着末⽇论成⻅进来的⼈，并直
⾔ Robinson 的核⼼批评是对的：⼀年前还管⽤的安全做法，已经防不住严重事故了。
哈佛教授、 OpenAI 研究员 Boaz Barak 表⽰， AI ⼏年⾥⾛完的路，相当于航空业从莱特兄弟⼀步
跨到载着⼏⼗亿⼈上天。

### Page 12

航空业的安全教训是吃了⼤亏才换来的，那时候节奏够慢，⽽现在， AI 没有这种奢侈。
但另⼀派「加速加速派」则⽕⼒全开，表⽰ AI 说不定就是照着末⽇论者写的东西照做的。

### Page 13

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

WS Global CIO Office

 The global bond sell-off is creating 
tactical and medium-term opportunities. 
In the US, September’s yield surge has 
improved the risk-reward in 10-year 
government bonds, presenting an 
attractive entry point for investors with 
a 6-12-month horizon.

to return as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs ease next year.

Editorial 
Opportunities from the bond sell-off  
Strategy summary: The global bond sell-off is creating tactical 
and medium-term opportunities. In the US, September’s yield 
surge has improved the risk -reward in 10 -year government 
bonds, presenting an attractive entry point for investors with a 
6-12-month horizon. We believe bond and money markets are 
overly hawkish about the Fed outlook, given disinflation is likely 
to return as impact of oil prices and tariffs ease next year. 
In equities, we stick to quality, especially sectors riding the AI -
led earnings boom. The upcoming Q3 earnings season is likely 
to show AI -investments are powering sectors beyond the 
technology and communications services, such as financials, 
materials and power and electrification. We remain constructive 
on the semiconductor and memory segments. We also expect 
the US Dollar to peak in the next few weeks along with short -
to-medium-term US bond yields. This creates opportunities for 
AUD corporate bonds, Emerging Market USD bonds and gold. 
Attractive risk -reward in US 10 -year government bonds.  
The US 10-year bond yield rose more than 50bps in September 
(biggest monthly rise since 2022) and 115bps this year, amid 
expectations of economic resilience, higher Fed rates, fuel -
driven inflation, AI-related capital demand and fiscal concerns. 
Valuations appear close to fair value, improving the 6-12-month 
risk-reward. Bond arithmetic suggests losses of less than 1% if 
the yield rises to 6%, vs. more than 10% gains if it falls to 4.5%. 
Resilient US economic activity, but softer -than-expected 
inflation. September data showed robust US employment 
(jobless claims, ADP) and core capital-goods orders. However, 
consumer confidence remains depressed, real disposable 
income is flat and households are drawing on savings to sustain 
spending. Against this divided backdrop, core PCE inflation - 
the Fed’s preferred gauge - was softer than expected at 0.2% 
m/m and 3.0% y/y. A revised method to measure PCE inflation 
also indicate that annual core PCE inflation was 35bps lower 
than previously reported, narrowing the gap with CPI inflation. 
Markets appear overly hawkish on the Fed. Money markets 
price about 85bps of tightening over the next 12 months. A 
strong labour market (September’s non -farm payrolls data is 
due tonight) may justify one or two further hikes, but aggressive 
tightening after the sharp rise in bond yields – which has 
already tightened financial conditions - could threaten financial 
stability. US small- and mid-cap equities have already fallen 8–
9% from recent peaks over the past couple of months, 
materially underperforming large caps, as energy and 
borrowing-cost concerns intensify. W hile some Fed speakers 
remain hawkish, we  agree with New York Fed President 
Williams’ that there is no urgency to tighten. We see little 
chance of an October Fed rate hike and expect inflation data 
for Sep-Nov determining whether the Fed moves in December. 
Stay with quality equities and AI beneficiaries.  US large 
caps have absorbed the rise in bond yields relatively well, 
supported by expectations of robust earnings growth led by AI-
related investment. We continue to favour quality companies 
and sectors benefiting from this AI cycle. Semiconductors and 
memory remain preferred exposures: structural AI demand 
should extend the memory upcycle, while long -term supply 
agreements provide better revenue visibility. The opportunity is 
also broadening beyond technology and communication 
services into financials, materials, power and electrification. 
Opportunities in AUD corporate bonds and gold.  The USD 
rebound alongside higher US yields offers a tactical opportunity 
to add higher -yielding Emerging Market USD and non -USD 
bonds. We remain positive on AUD corporate bonds, which 
offer a yield premium over comparable US credit. Our 
constructive medium-term AUD/USD view could add returns for 
USD-based investors over the next 6 –12 months. We would 
also use gold’s recent pullback to add exposure, supported by 
sustained Emerging Market central bank demand. We continue 
to view USD 4,000/oz as important technical support for gold.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank

Top client questions 
  Do you expect more RBA rate hikes after its less hawkish 
guidance? What is your view on the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We expect the RBA to leave rates unchanged, but 
retain the option to hike again should inflation intensify. We 
maintain our medium-term constructive AUD view and maintain 
our opportunistically bullish AUD corporate bonds idea. 
Rationale: The RBA’s forward guidance remains data dependent, 
explicitly allowing further tightening if needed as inflation remains too 
high.  At 4.60%, Australia’s cash rate is currently 60–85bp above the 
Fed’s 3.75–4.00% range. Thus, even with our two additional 25bp s 
Fed hikes expectation, Australian rates would hold a modest positive 
rate differential. We expect the AUD to strengthen once the Fed 
signals that US rates are near their peak, which should allow resilient 
Australian fundamentals to reassert themselves. Key risks are 
persistently high US yields, weaker China growth or a sharper 
deterioration in global risk sentiment. 
We believe risk-reward has become more attractive for AUD bonds 
following the bear -flattening of the AUD yield curve. Markets are 
already pricing in one more RBA hike through end-2027, presenting 
an attractive opportunity to earn a yield even as increasingly 
restrictive financial conditions reinforc e disinflation over time. 
Meanwhile, the positive interest rate differential with the US supports 
the currency outlook, supporting the case for AUD bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  What does the incoming US data tell us about the path for 
US 10-year bond yields from here? 
Our view: The risk -reward in US 10 -year yields has turned 
attractive following their move higher amid incoming US data. 
Rationale: Recent US data has indeed started to show softer-than-
expected inflation. However, market technicals are likely temporarily 
delaying a move lower in bond yields, with reports suggesting the  
rise in yields is creating forced selling amongst hedge funds and real 
estate investment trusts. Nevertheless, we do not expect this to be 
an extended selloff. We have initiated an opportunistic bullish idea 
on US 10-year government bonds this week and continue to believe 
this idea offers an attractive risk/reward on a 6-12-month horizon as 
yields become attractive enough to entice buyer flow to return. On a 
broader allocation basis, we retain our structural preference for 3-7-
year maturities in bonds. 
— Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in the GBP ahead of the UK 
budget announcement next month? 
Our view: We maintain a medium-term constructive GBP view, 
but keep near-term exposure measured ahead of the 28 October 
Budget. We would view any pre -Budget weakness as an 
opportunity to gradually build GBP exposure. 
Rationale: The government’s decision to adjust the pension triple 
lock from 2030 is an early indication of greater willingness to tackle 
longer-term spending pressures, reinforcing Chancellor Healey’s 
commitment to fiscal discipline and meeting the fiscal rules. Markets 
are likely to scrutinise the Budget closely for evidence that this 
discipline extends to near -term tax and spending decisions. A 
credible Budget should help contain pressure on UK government 
bond yields and support GBP. Any fiscal slippage remains a key risk.  
Near term, we prefer measured GBP exposure as elevated US yields 
and repricing towards further Fed tightening could continue to 
support the USD. Over the medium term, we expect GBP to rebound 
as the Fed eventually signals the peak of its tightening cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How would different US mid-term election outcome 
scenarios affect the outlook for US equities? 
Our view: We believe election outcomes will have a limited 
impact over the next 12 months. We remain positive on US 
equities driven by fundamental earnings growth.  
Rationale: Over the past 50 years, the ruling US President’s party 
has retained control of both chambers of Congress on only two 
occasions (1978 and 2002). All other elections result ed in an 
opposition sweep or a divided government.  
Based on the latest prediction markets, it appears that an opposition 
sweep (both the House and Senate flips from Republican to 
Democrats) is more likely than a divided government ( in which the 
House flips to Democrats , while the Senate remains under 
Republican control). A scenario where Republicans retain a majority 
is least likely per prediction markets. 
While mid -term elections may introduce near -term volatility, we 
caution against overreaction. Historically, the average return for the 
S&P 500 following an opposition sweep is 17% over the next 12 
months, but the average return following a divided government is not 
significantly worse, at 15%. We prefer to base our investment views 
on fundamental earnings growth, where investor attention should 
focus on the upcoming Q3 earnings season. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 2 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the outlook for  the memory chip and broader 
semiconductors industry? 
Our view: We remain positive on semiconductors and memory 
chips. Structural AI demand should prolong the memory 
upcycle while long-term agreements improve revenue visibility. 
Rationale: Recent industry commentary points to tighter memory 
supply-demand conditions through 2028, reinforcing our view that AI 
demand is likely to extend the memory upcycle. Furthermore, adoption 
of long-term agreements improves revenue and earnings visibility. 
Despite debates around “Pacing the Frontier”, we have seen a flurry 
of new model releases, along with the introduction of new consumer 
AI agents capable of comparing prices online and making 
purchases. These are early days, but the path is open for a business-
to-agent (B2A) economy where businesses increasingly interact with 
AI assistants acting on customers’ behalf. As these applications are 
increasingly inference intensive, wider adoption should support 
demand for memory, with its share of global semiconductor 
revenues projected to rise from 30.7% in 2025 to 66.7% in 2027. 
Against this backdrop, we maintain our Opportunistic idea on Global 
Semiconductors. Given memory  chips’ cyclical nature, investors 
should avoid overconcentration in memory names and maintain a 
diversified exposure across Big Tech and semiconductors. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Does a US bond yield stress test raise the risk to your 
constructive view on US equities? 
Our view: We have a target for the S&P 500 index of 8400 over 
the next 12 months. A scenario of a sustained rise in the US 10-
year government bond yield to 5.6% could lead to negative 
returns from the current S&P500 level (7,666). 
Rationale: Our base case 12-month target for the S&P 500 is 8,400, 
which assumes 2027 S&P 500 EPS of USD 420 and a 20x forward 
P/E multiple. A rise in bond yields would have an impact on equity 
valuations, as a higher discount rate reduces the present value of 
future earnings. W e stress -test our target under different 10 -year 
yield and earnings scenarios.  
A rise in bond yields would arguably be associated with a stronger 
growth environment and hence, even higher earnings. However, 
assuming unchanged EPS at our base case of USD 420, a 
(sustained) rise in the 10 -year bond yield to 5.6% would lead to 
negative returns from the current level  if not offset by a further 
improvement in earnings growth . However, in o ur base case  we 
expect the 10-year yield is more likely to fall from here. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 332: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors

WS Global CIO Office

### Page 2

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  2

India’s financial coming of age has created a generation of investors at a 
distinctive crossroads: deeply connected to one of the world’s fastest -growing 
economies, yet increasingly aware that portfolios concentrated in a single market 
can accumulate risks over time. 
This report discusses the challenges faced by two distinct ‘Global Indian’ investor 
profiles. The first is the onshore Indian investor: a resident Indian whose wealth 
is largely denominated in domestic equities and fixed income, but who may still treat 
global markets as an occasional satellite allocation rather than a core portfolio 
component. The second is the offshore Indian investor : a non -resident Indian 
(NRI) based outside India with full access to global capital markets, yet who may 
be under-allocated to the growth story unfolding in their country of origin. Because 
global benchmarks understate India’s economic importance, investors seeking 
more meaningful participation in its growth story may consider allocating above 
benchmark levels, with NRIs’ potential stronger  affinity with India providing an 
additional reason to make this adjustment. 
For both groups, the starting point is the same: a concentrated bias towards either 
Indian or global markets can create risks that diversification can help mitigate 
without requiring investors to abandon their core convictions. For onshore Indian 
investors, global assets can provide structural currency diversification and access 
to sectors that are under -represented in Indian indices. Our Capital Market 
Assumptions (CMAs)  illustrate the potential portfolio benefits: increasing  
the allocation to global assets from 0% to 15% raises expected returns while 
reducing expected volatility meaningfully. As a result, the portfolio’s risk -adjusted 
returns improve. 
For offshore Indian investors, India offers a real yield premium, a rising weight in global indices and long-term growth that remains 
meaningfully under -represented in global benchmarks. A historical perspective illustrates the opportunity: since 2001,  
Indian equities have delivered over 10% annualised returns in USD terms, outperforming Developed Market equities over the 
same period. 
Onshore and offshore Indian investors may share the same connection to India, but they face very different portfolio challenges. 
There are key dividing lines that determine how each investor should think about diversification, risk management and the rol e 
of Indian assets within a broader investment portfolio.

Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Sylvain Huard 
Head of Asset Allocation 
Hannah Chew 
Portfolio Strategist

1 Two investor profiles, one shared 
opportunity

### Page 3

InvesTips: Roots and routes – A framework for Global Indian investors  |  September 2026  3

Base currency –  
the first and most 
important divide 
 The onshore Indian investor earns, spends, saves and ultimately retires in rupees (INR). Their 
relevant return is, therefore, an INR return. If the INR depreciates against the US dollar (USD), 
foreign assets rise in INR terms and can act as a natural hedge . If the INR appreciates, those 
same assets deliver lower INR returns than their local -currency performance would suggest. 
For the offshore investor, the currency problem is reversed. Their benchmark is typically USD-
based, and Indian markets are the exposure that introduces exchange-rate risk. A strong year 
for Indian equities in INR terms may look less compelling once translated back into USD at an 
unfavourable exchange rate.

Regulatory access –  
different constraints, 
different routes 
 The onshore Indian investor operates within the Liberalised Remittance Scheme (LRS) 
framework. Depending on their circumstances, this can offer meaningful or constrained access 
to global markets. Fund -of-funds and international exchange -traded funds (ETFs)  can also 
offer alternative access routes, as opposed to direct securities alone.  
For the offshore Indian investor, the challenge is less about access to India than choosing the 
most appropriate investment route. They can generally invest through onshore accounts, while 
offshore-listed funds and ETFs may offer a more convenient alternat ive. The preferred 
structure will depend on their residency, tax domicile and product eligibility, making careful 
planning important even where access itself is relatively broad. 
As an example, India’s Gujarat International Finance Tec -City (GIFT City), home to the 
country’s International Financial Services Centre (IFSC), is one potential implementation route 
available to both onshore and offshore Indian investors. For offshore investors, GIFT City offers 
an efficient way to access India-linked and global investment products with little or no need for 
INR conversion, while allowing foreign -currency repatriation. For onshore investors, it can 
facilitate USD -denominated exposure throu gh foreign -currency accounts under the LRS, 
providing a domestically regulated, yet globally oriented platform for portfolio diversification.

Portfolio risk –  
concentration on 
one side, under-
allocation on the 
other 
 The onshore Indian investor’s core risk is concentration and complacency: a portfolio almost 
entirely exposed to India’s domestic economic cycle, policy environment and sectoral 
composition. This is not a prediction that these will deteriorate, as India’s structural growth story 
remains compelling. Rather, this implies the risk is structural when all equity exposure sits 
within a single market, causing the portfolio to react simultaneously to the same domestic 
catalysts. By contrast, the offshore Indian investor faces the opposite problem: under-allocation 
to one of the world’s fastest-growing economies, driven partly by access frictions and partly by 
the difficulty of sizing a home-market allocation through a global investment framework.
